I know other people might not see it that way, but I can’t shake the feeling that it’s personal and that they’re watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events happening around you, such as things in the church bulletin or people's remarks, are specifically directed at you or have a hidden meaning meant only for you to understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. It feels more like certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and little things in the bulletin or what they say have messages about me. I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing a bit better.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know I’ve said it already, but that’s really what’s been bothering me. Even when I’m having a better day, I still feel like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages or actions by others are part of a coordinated effort or plan directed at you, or do you think they are happening without any organized intent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s a coordinated plan exactly. It feels more like certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and little things in the bulletin or what they say have messages about me. I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing a bit better.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that random events or things people say in your daily life, even outside of church or the walking club, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s with church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most—like people are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Outside of that, I don’t know… I wouldn’t say it happens as much, but that feeling is still hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages or hidden meanings extend beyond just the church bulletin or walking club conversations, like even in TV shows, news, or other everyday events?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or the news, no. It’s mostly the church bulletin and things people say at church or in the walking club. That’s what I came in about, really—because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the things people say or write, like in the church bulletin or during the walking club, are specifically meant for you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s how it feels to me. I know other people might not notice it or think anything of it, but I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that things in the bulletin or things they say have little messages meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or special meanings are only happening in certain places, like church or the walking club, or do they also happen in other parts of your life with people who aren’t part of those groups?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly in those certain places, church and the walking club. I can’t really say it happens much with other people outside of that. But even on better days, I still feel like some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or special meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are part of a larger, hidden reality that others are not aware of?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole hidden reality. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Even when I’m doing a bit better, that feeling is still there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F33.2, F44.89\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what's been troubling you and what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because even when I’m having a pretty okay day, I can’t shake this feeling that people are watching me. Mostly people at church, and some in my walking club. It feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin or slipping things into what they say. I know it may sound strange, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things people say or actions they take are directed at you or have a special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do feel like certain things are meant for me. Like at church, something in the bulletin will seem like it’s hinting at me, or someone in the walking club will say something that feels like it has a message behind it. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t really shake the feeling that they’re watching me or talking around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things people do or say, such as events in the church bulletin or casual remarks, have a direct and personal meaning for you that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s how it feels to me. Like when I read something in the church bulletin, or someone in the walking club makes a casual comment, it can feel like there’s a little message in it about me. I know other people might not see it that way, but I can’t shake the feeling that it’s personal and that they’re watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events happening around you, such as things in the church bulletin or people's remarks, are specifically directed at you or have a hidden meaning meant only for you to understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know I’ve said it already, but that’s really what’s been bothering me. Even when I’m having a better day, I still feel like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages or actions by others are part of a coordinated effort or plan directed at you, or do you think they are happening without any organized intent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s a coordinated plan exactly. It feels more like certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and little things in the bulletin or what they say have messages about me. I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing a bit better.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that random events or things people say in your daily life, even outside of church or the walking club, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s with church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most—like people are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Outside of that, I don’t know… I wouldn’t say it happens as much, but that feeling is still hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages or hidden meanings extend beyond just the church bulletin or walking club conversations, like even in TV shows, news, or other everyday events?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or the news, no. It’s mostly the church bulletin and things people say at church or in the walking club. That’s what I came in about, really—because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the things people say or write, like in the church bulletin or during the walking club, are specifically meant for you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s how it feels to me. I know other people might not notice it or think anything of it, but I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that things in the bulletin or things they say have little messages meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or special meanings are only happening in certain places, like church or the walking club, or do they also happen in other parts of your life with people who aren’t part of those groups?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly in those certain places, church and the walking club. I can’t really say it happens much with other people outside of that. But even on better days, I still feel like some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or special meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are part of a larger, hidden reality that others are not aware of?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole hidden reality. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Even when I’m doing a bit better, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or meanings are only happening in those specific groups, or do you sometimes feel that there's a bigger picture or hidden truth that connects them, even if others don't see it?\n\nPatient: It feels mostly tied to those groups, church and the walking club. I don’t really notice it much in other parts of my life. It’s more at church and with the walking club, like some of them are watching me or slipping little messages about me into the bulletin or in what they say. Even when I’m having a better day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, without any doubt, that certain people at church or in your walking club are watching you and sending you hidden messages, even though others don't seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say without any doubt, but it feels very real to me.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F33.2, F44.89\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what's been troubling you and what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because even when I’m having a pretty okay day, I can’t shake this feeling that people are watching me. Mostly people at church, and some in my walking club. It feels like they’re putting little messages about me in the church bulletin or slipping things into what they say. I know it may sound strange, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things people say or actions they take are directed at you or have a special meaning for you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do feel like certain things are meant for me. Like at church, something in the bulletin will seem like it’s hinting at me, or someone in the walking club will say something that feels like it has a message behind it. Even when I’m having a better day, I can’t really shake the feeling that they’re watching me or talking around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things people do or say, such as events in the church bulletin or casual remarks, have a direct and personal meaning for you that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s how it feels to me. Like when I read something in the church bulletin, or someone in the walking club makes a casual comment, it can feel like there’s a little message in it about me. I know other people might not see it that way, but I can’t shake the feeling that it’s personal and that they’re watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events happening around you, such as things in the church bulletin or people's remarks, are specifically directed at you or have a hidden meaning meant only for you to understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know I’ve said it already, but that’s really what’s been bothering me. Even when I’m having a better day, I still feel like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages or actions by others are part of a coordinated effort or plan directed at you, or do you think they are happening without any organized intent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s a coordinated plan exactly. It feels more like certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and little things in the bulletin or what they say have messages about me. I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing a bit better.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that random events or things people say in your daily life, even outside of church or the walking club, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s with church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most—like people are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Outside of that, I don’t know… I wouldn’t say it happens as much, but that feeling is still hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages or hidden meanings extend beyond just the church bulletin or walking club conversations, like even in TV shows, news, or other everyday events?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or the news, no. It’s mostly the church bulletin and things people say at church or in the walking club. That’s what I came in about, really—because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the things people say or write, like in the church bulletin or during the walking club, are specifically meant for you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s how it feels to me. I know other people might not notice it or think anything of it, but I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that things in the bulletin or things they say have little messages meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or special meanings are only happening in certain places, like church or the walking club, or do they also happen in other parts of your life with people who aren’t part of those groups?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly in those certain places, church and the walking club. I can’t really say it happens much with other people outside of that. But even on better days, I still feel like some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or special meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are part of a larger, hidden reality that others are not aware of?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole hidden reality. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Even when I’m doing a bit better, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or meanings are only happening in those specific groups, or do you sometimes feel that there's a bigger picture or hidden truth that connects them, even if others don't see it?\n\nPatient: It feels mostly tied to those groups, church and the walking club. I don’t really think of it as some big hidden truth, but even on better days I can’t shake it… like certain people there are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that things people say or do in other parts of your life, outside of church or the walking club, have any special meaning for you, or is it mostly just those two groups?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s just those two groups. I don’t really notice it much in other parts of my life. It’s more at church and with the walking club, like some of them are watching me or slipping little messages about me into the bulletin or in what they say. Even when I’m having a better day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, without any doubt, that certain people at church or in your walking club are watching you and sending you hidden messages, even though others don't seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say without any doubt, but it feels very real to me. Even on better days, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the messages or special meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club are part of a pattern where things around you are directly related to you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it feels like a pattern, mostly in those places. I know it may not make sense to other people, but I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that strangers or people you don’t know are also sending you messages or watching you, or is it only people you personally know, like in your church or walking club?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly people I know from church or the walking club. I don’t usually feel that way about strangers. I know it may sound odd, but I can’t shake that feeling, even on better days.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are part of a larger, organized effort that you can’t fully explain, or do you think it’s more of a personal experience that others just don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it a big organized effort. Even when I’m having a better day, I still feel like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages or actions by others are part of a coordinated effort or plan directed at you, or do you think they are happening without any organized intent?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s a coordinated plan exactly. It feels more like certain people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and little things in the bulletin or what they say have messages about me. I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing a bit better.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that random events or things people say in your daily life, even outside of church or the walking club, have a special meaning or connection to you personally?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s with church and the walking club. That’s where I notice it the most—like people are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Outside of that, I don’t know… I wouldn’t say it happens as much, but that feeling is still hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages or hidden meanings extend beyond just the church bulletin or walking club conversations, like even in TV shows, news, or other everyday events?\n\nPatient: Not so much with TV or the news, no. It’s mostly the church bulletin and things people say at church or in the walking club. That’s what I came in about, really—because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t shake the feeling that some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the things people say or write, like in the church bulletin or during the walking club, are specifically meant for you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s how it feels to me. I know other people might not notice it or think anything of it, but I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that things in the bulletin or things they say have little messages meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or special meanings are only happening in certain places, like church or the walking club, or do they also happen in other parts of your life with people who aren’t part of those groups?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly in those certain places, church and the walking club. I can’t really say it happens much with other people outside of that. But even on better days, I still feel like some of them are watching me or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or special meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are part of a larger, hidden reality that others are not aware of?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole hidden reality. It’s more that I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. Even when I’m doing a bit better, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these messages or meanings are only happening in those specific groups, or do you sometimes feel that there's a bigger picture or hidden truth that connects them, even if others don't see it?\n\nPatient: It feels mostly tied to those groups, church and the walking club. I don’t really think of it as some big hidden truth, but even on better days I can’t shake it… like certain people there are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that things people say or do in other parts of your life, outside of church or the walking club, have any special meaning for you, or is it mostly just those two groups?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s just those two groups. I don’t really notice it much in other parts of my life. It’s more at church and with the walking club, like some of them are watching me or slipping little messages about me into the bulletin or in what they say. Even when I’m having a better day, that feeling is still there.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, without any doubt, that certain people at church or in your walking club are watching you and sending you hidden messages, even though others don't seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say without any doubt, but it feels very real to me. Even on better days, I can’t shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the messages or special meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club are part of a pattern where things around you are directly related to you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it feels like a pattern, mostly in those places. Like the bulletin or something someone says in the walking club feels like it’s about me, even if nobody else seems to notice it. I know it may not make sense to other people, but I can’t really shake that feeling, even when I’m doing better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that strangers or people you don’t know are also sending you messages or watching you, or is it only people you personally know, like in your church or walking club?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly people I know from church or the walking club. I don’t usually feel that way about strangers. It’s more when I’m there, or thinking about what they said, or seeing something in the bulletin, and I get this feeling it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these messages or meanings in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are something only you can understand, and that others don’t see them the way you do?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I’m the one who notices it. Other people don’t seem to see it that way, but to me it feels like the bulletin or something someone says is meant for me somehow. I know it may sound odd, but I can’t shake that feeling, even on better days.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages or meanings you notice in the church bulletin or walking club conversations are part of a larger, organized effort that you can’t fully explain, or do you think it’s more of a personal experience that others just don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it a big organized effort. It feels more personal to me. Like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and then little things show up in the bulletin or in what they say that feel like they’re about me. I can’t fully explain it, but even on better days I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe, with strong conviction, that certain people at church or in the walking club are sending you hidden messages, even though there’s no clear evidence that others can see or confirm?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do feel pretty strongly that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that little things in the bulletin or in what they say are messages about me. I know I can’t really prove it, and other people don’t seem to see it, but even on better days I can’t shake that feeling."
